# Historical checkpoint - 2026-10-13 18:49 Vancouver: upload HTTP 503 server repair deployed

- User reported HTTP 503 on upload, describing it as immediate. Cloudflare Workers Logs show the exact failures at 18:40:32 and 18:42:59 PDT: `Worker exceeded CPU time limit.` Both were `/upload/chunk` requests (offsets 83886080 and 8388608), on worker version 157148fa. Health/list/start worked during diagnosis. This is a confirmed server CPU-limit failure, not a missing game branch or custom-head import error.
- Server now advertises 1 MiB chunks (previously 8 MiB), which the existing released Studio honors automatically at upload start. Known-length bounded bodies use native arrayBuffer reading instead of accumulating and recopying every network fragment in JavaScript. The server still accepts 8 MiB for already-started sessions; size caps, signed tickets, secrets and stored campaigns remain unchanged. No new Studio installer is needed for this repair.
- Existing worker https://t2tcampaings.shaespring14.workers.dev deployed through Cloudflare UI, active 100% version **f51ba17e**. Local worker source matched pasted editor contents before deployment. Eleven focused protocol checks passed, including exact bytes, 1 MiB negotiation, prior 8 MiB sessions, repeated chunks, bad size/offset/ticket rejection and finish.
- Live verification accepted **64 consecutive 1 MiB chunks (64 MiB total)** with no retries or errors, then correctly acknowledged a repeated last chunk. The diagnostic session was not finished, so no public test campaign was created. Receipt: `build/installer/worker-cpu-fix-verification.json`; deployed proof: `build/installer/worker-cpu-fix-deployed.png`. User's upload retry after this server repair remains unconfirmed.
- The public community list already contains the user's earlier `NHL - Canucks Road to the Cup - Anonymous` upload: 319745461 bytes, completed 18:16:32 PDT. Preserve it; do not publish duplicates on the user's behalf.
- Worker-only source commit **f602814c7767efdf1d75e8e2fdf9567e0a2278e8** pushed and verified on origin/main, clean mirror. ## Source changes prepared, NOT installed

- `src/CampaignCore/MapLayout.cs`: persists `NativeRandomWeights` separately from `RandomWeights`; unchanged native choices do not become a custom runtime override. Known empty metadata stays distinct from absent metadata.

The August instructions below are historical and do not describe the current working tree.





























> **START HERE — updated 2026-08-19, session 22.**














> Historical baseline; superseded by the September handoff linked above.





























## Session 22 (2026-08-19) — game re-baseline, offside, Play Now looks, relics





























Everything below is **built, deployed and hash-verified at 2.1.35** (all four














version files bumped, CHANGELOG written, installer + Linux zip rebuilt).














**Nothing has been launched. Not one line of it has been seen running.**





























### Do this first





























Launch the game once. That single launch does three jobs:





























1. Regenerates `BepInEx/interop/` against the new build (see below).














2. Regenerates `T2T_Dumps/` — including `_gui_data/_export_id_map.txt`, which














   now needs a **`[relics]`** section for the Play Now team export to carry














   relics at all. **Without a launch, exported teams still get zero relics.**














3. Then run `python _gen_game_data.py` to refresh the Creator's talent / relic /














   ability lists against the Aug-18 build.





























Then read the log for the lines each fix prints — they are named per fix below.





























### The game updated on 2026-08-18





























`GameAssembly.dll`, `data.unity3d` and `global-metadata.dat` are all dated














Aug 18 21:30. **`dump_2026-08-18/` is now the only valid dump.** The update also














wiped `BepInEx/core` and `BepInEx/patchers` (`winhttp.dll` and `dotnet/`














survived); they were restored from `installer/bepinex/`. Expect the same after














the next game update — the mod simply does not load until they are back.





























Checked against the new dump and **unchanged**: every Harmony target the mod














patches, and `ForwardData` field-for-field. `TeamData` gained a private














`descriptionLoc`; `GameplaySettingsData` gained `bodycheckInterruptsPokecheck`














(which shifted `offside` 0x2B→0x2C and `offsidePenalty` 0x2C→0x30 — harmless,














the mod goes through properties).





























`Il2CppDumper.exe` needs .NET 7, which is not installed. Run it with














`$env:DOTNET_ROLL_FORWARD = "LatestMajor"` and `RequireAnyKey: false` in its














`config.json` (both already set).





























### 1. `Talent 'Linesman' not found` — fixed





























`FindTalentOnce` only ever searched `TalentRepository.talents`, which is the














**random-reward pool**, not the set of talents that exist. A real, grantable














talent outside that pool was unreachable. It now falls back to every loaded














`Talent` asset — the way `FindRelic` right below it always has — matching exact














name, case-insensitive name, `(Clone)`-stripped name, then localized name.





























Log line when it works: `[Remix] Talent 'Linesman' resolved as asset 'Linesman'














(not in the repo's pool list)`. If it still misses, the asset genuinely is not














loaded, which is a different problem from the one this fixed.





























### 2. Campaign-wide offside — new





























`Offside = yes` and optional `Offside Penalty = Whistle | Lose Puck | Knockout`














in `campaign.txt`, with a checkbox and dropdown in the campaign editor.














`OffsideMatch` is modelled directly on `SoccerMatch` — `BeginMatch` from both














LaunchMatch prefixes, `Tick()` from `PreviewAssetsRunner.Update`, `End()` from














the match-end and run-end patches.





























**Do not "simplify" this to `GameplaySettings.Offside = true`.** Disassembling














`set_Offside` in the Aug-18 binary shows it calls `SaveSettings()` and then














jumps to `OffsideController.Refresh()` — it rewrites `GameplaySettings.es3`, so














it would permanently change the player's own Play Now setting. The mod uses














`OffsideController.AddForceSource(token)` / `RemoveForceSource(token)` instead,














which is what `LinesmanController` uses and touches nothing on disk.





























`Offside Penalty` is the one part that DOES persist (same setter), so it is only














written when the campaign names one, and the previous value is saved and














restored on match end and run end. Leave it unset and nothing is touched.





























To verify: `[Offside] Boss node: offside is enforced for this match`, then














`[Offside] Rule armed for this match (active=True)`, then `[Offside] Force














source removed` when the match ends. If the second line never appears the poll














is finding no `OffsideController` — log what `FindObjectsOfTypeAll` returns














before changing anything.





























### 3. Play Now uniform/colour overrides — fixed with a sidecar





























**The root cause is the save format, and it is worth stating plainly so nobody














re-investigates the Creator.** `Tape2Tape.Customization.ForwardDataModel` has














fields for exactly six look values: `headSkin`, `bodySkin`, `bodyAwaySkin`,














`stickSkin`, `logoSkin`, `numberSkin`. `ForwardData` — the thing that renders —














has eleven more (`helmetSkin`, `helmetAwaySkin`, `glassesSkin`, `bicepSkin`,














`gloveSkin`, `pantsSkin`, `skateSkin`, each away variant, `colorSchemes`).














**Unknown JSON keys are dropped on load, so writing them into the same file














does nothing.** That is why a player looked right in a campaign and wrong in














Play Now. `GoalieDataModel` is fine for skins; only colours fall through.





























The Creator now writes those fields to














`<mod folder>/play_now_overrides/<id>.txt`, in the same `key = value` format














campaign player files use, and `PlayNowOverrides` (Plugin.cs) postfixes














`ForwardDataAdapter.ConvertToForwardData` / `GoalieDataAdapter.ConvertToGoaltenderData`














to stamp them on. Both sides go through the **same** parser and the same














`ApplyPlayerLookOnly` the campaign path uses, so they cannot drift apart again.





























- Registered **before** the DEFAULT MODE return in `Plugin.Load` — an exported














  Play Now player is not part of any campaign.














- Slots left on `standard` are deliberately **not** written: that is the game's














  own default and writing it would override a value with itself every load.














- `tests/test_play_now_export.py` pins the round trip, including that every key














  the Creator can emit is one `ApplyPlayerField` actually handles. A key it














  doesn't handle is read, ignored, and lost silently — run that test after














  touching either side.





























Log line: `[PlayNow] Uniform overrides applied to '<name>' (<id>)`.





























### 4. Level-2 relics never applied — fixed





























The relic picker lists relics by **display name**, and the game's own display














name for an upgraded relic ends in `LVL2`. `GiveRelic` always asked `FindRelic`














for level 1, and `FindRelic` filters on level — so every `LVL2` relic in every














campaign was filtered out and dropped **without an error**. New helper














`PatchBossLaunchMatch.LevelFromRelicName` reads the level from the name and is














used by both the team path and the custom-squad starting-relics path.





























### 5. Play Now teams exported with no relics — fixed





























`TeamDataModel.relics` is a `List<string>` of relic ids; the Creator had no id














source and hardcoded `"relics": []`. The DLL's `_export_id_map.txt` now has a














`[relics]` section keyed by display name, and `_relic_ids()` resolves it.














**Needs the game launch above to exist at all.**





























### 6. Appearance-only custom-squad slots were skipped — fixed





























`ApplyPlayerTeamConfig`'s `hasAny` gate asked only about identity, stats,














talents and ability, so a slot configured purely for looks matched nothing and














was skipped. It now falls through to `ApplyPlayerLookOnly`.





























**Not** the full `ApplyPlayerConfig`: that writes the config's absolute stats,














and an appearance-only file leaves those at 50, which would flatten whichever














draft pick or superstar the game seated in that slot.

## Traps that have already cost this project real time





























- **Helmet visibility is `Plugin.HandleNoHelmetSentinel`, and it is TWO things:**














  register the head in `HeadsWithoutHelmets` **and** blank














  `helmetSkin`/`helmetAwaySkin`. Doing only the first does nothing visible, and














  that half-implementation once produced a confident, wrong "this mechanism is a














  dead end" conclusion. **When a behaviour already works somewhere in this














  codebase, read and copy that code before declaring anything impossible.**














- **`X` and `X_Helmet` are a PAIR** — the same character bare and drawn to sit














  under a helmet — never duplicates. Collapsing them threw away half of every














  face and cost most of session 19.














- **Every "Basic Squad ships like this" constant in `PatchCustomSquads` is a














  template assumption.** A hardcoded Angus slot 3 is what broke the GM template














  the day the setting shipped.














- **In the exporter, "absent" means "needs rendering."** Any decision NOT to














  render something must be recorded where the queue reads it, or the inventory














  scan re-queues it forever. A version bump does **not** delete images — they are














  keyed by `File.Exists`; `PurgeRenderedArt` exists for exactly this and the trap














  has still fired three times. `PreviewAssets.ExporterVersion` and














  `preview_cache.PREVIEW_CACHE_VERSION` must always match (both `10` today).














- **Never walk Spine `Skin.Attachments` through IL2CPP reflection** — hard crash,














  0xC0000005, try/catch will not save you.














- **Verify renders by CONTENT and by eye.** Counts, hashes and pixel thresholds














  have each passed on garbage in this project. Compositing the exported PNGs into














  one contact sheet and looking at it settled every face question there was.

## Session 21 (2026-08-02) — Squad Template setting





























_Run ONCE with the GM template — it broke (see below), the fix is deployed and














hash-verified, and the FIX has **not** been verified in game yet._





























`Squad Template = <squad>` in `campaign.txt`, with a dropdown under Maps in the














Creator's campaign settings. It decides which base squad a CUSTOM squad is cloned














from. Empty / "Basic" behaves exactly as before, so every existing campaign is














untouched.





























- **Not the same setting as `Maps`.** Maps picks the map layout; this picks the














  starting roster shape, relics and unlock state the clone inherits. They are














  independent — a campaign can use the GM maps with a Basic template or vice














  versa.














- The clone site is `PatchCustomSquads` (Plugin.cs, was a hardcoded search for














  `"Basic Squad"`). It now looks for the requested name, tolerates the missing














  "Squad" suffix (`General Manager` matches `General Manager Squad`), and














  **falls back to Basic** on a typo or a squad this install lacks rather than














  leaving the campaign with no custom squads.














**It shipped broken and was fixed the same session — read this before touching














`SeatUserStarterAtAngusSlot`.** Choosing the GM template started the run with














`fwd[0] = 'General McShaggy'` and slots 1-4 null: none of the user's players were














seated. `SeatUserStarterAtAngusSlot` had `const int angusIdx = 3` — Basic Squad's














Angus index — so with the GM squad, whose Angus is at index **0**, the keep-slot














was never found, McShaggy was never replaced or neutralised, and he survived into














the run wearing his own name and talents. The keep-slot is now FOUND: by














`skaterCategory` containing "Angus", else the template's single non-null forward.














The LD-is-the-keep-slot assumption went with it — the config slot checked is now














whichever one owns `angusIdx`.





























**Generalise the lesson: every "Basic ships like this" constant is a














template assumption.** Adding a template setting turns each one into a bug. Slot














3, the LD mapping and the one-forward shape were all of them.





























- **The 5-forward-slot rule is enforced and must stay.** Two-line squads














  ("Lines Meta") have ten forward slots — line 1 at 0-4, line 2 at 5-9 — and the














  layout collides with the single-line model the rest of the mod assumes. A














  template with more than five slots is rejected with a warning and Basic is














  used. Those squads are also left out of the Creator's dropdown.

A **Soccer** checkbox on every node in the campaign screen's Map Opponents grid,














next to that node's opponent dropdown. Ticking it plays that one game with the














soccer ball on the soccer field.





























- **Storage:** `node_soccer.txt` in the campaign folder, same coordinate syntax as














  `assignments.txt` (`Map 1 / Layer 2 / Node 0 = yes`). Deliberately its own file














  — the assignments parser is load-bearing on both sides and there was no reason














  to reopen it. Absent file = nothing to do, so old campaigns are untouched.














  Round-trip tested offline. `Plugin.TryReadNodeCoords` is now shared by both














  loaders so they can never disagree on what a coordinate means.














- **The ball:** `PatchPuckManager` already existed to REPLACE the soccer ball with














  a puck; a marked node runs the same trick the other way (`regularPuck =














  soccerBall`) and wins over the campaign-wide "replace soccer ball" setting,














  which it is deliberately overriding. `InitializePuckVisuals` picks by team, so














  pointing `regularPuck` at the ball covers both sides.














- **The field:** `Arena.IceSetup.SwitchToSoccerIce()`, called by reflection — it














  returns a `UniTask` and referencing that assembly only to discard the handle














  buys nothing. Driven from `PreviewAssetsRunner.Update` (the mod's only














  DontDestroyOnLoad per-frame hook) because `IceSetup` does not exist until the














  match scene has loaded, and switching from inside its own `Initialize` fights














  the setup already in flight. Polls at 2 Hz, once, then stops.














- **The decision point** is `BossMapNode.LaunchMatch` / `EliteMapNode.LaunchMatch`














  — both already patched — which is where the node, and therefore its














  coordinates, is known.

match uses the soccer ball and field` then `[Soccer] Rink switched to the soccer














field` in the log. If the ball is right but the rink is not, the poll is finding














no `IceSetup` — log what `FindObjectsOfTypeAll` returns before changing anything.












































## Session 19 (2026-08-02) — faces export with no helmet, and nothing is filtered





























_Built, deployed, DLL hash-verified against a clean build. **Not launched — the














render side is unverified.** Version still 2.1.34; nothing bumped or released._





























**The complaint:** exported faces come with helmets on them, and faces were going














missing from the Creator.





























**The cause, and a correction to session 18 §2.** Session 18 concluded that the














bare twin of a face carries a helmet baked into its own art, and that the














`_Helmet` twin is the correct one. That is backwards. The bright red helmet it














measured on bare faces is the game's own helmet PIECE, drawn on top because the














donor skater wears one, in raw key colour — the capture uses a replacement














`UI/Default` material that does not run the palette remap (§1 of session 18's own














correction). The face job varied only the Face field, so the donor's helmet














stayed on for every single face.





























**The fix.** The game decides helmet visibility from the HEAD skin, not the helmet














field — `ForwardDataExtensions.HeadsWithoutHelmets`, the same mechanism














`Plugin.RegisterFaceAsHelmetless` drives for a configured player. `BeginRender`














now appends the face being rendered to that array before the refresh, and














`FinishRender`'s `finally` puts the ORIGINAL array back. Scoped deliberately:














`RegisterFaceAsHelmetless` appends permanently, which is right for one player and














would leave every skater in the session bare-headed across 200-odd faces.





























**Nothing is filtered any more.**





























- The post-render "this face has a helmet, do not export it" skip is gone. It now














  only COUNTS faces that still show key-coloured pixels and reports them on the














  Layers line. That count is the verification instrument: **non-zero means the














  suppression is not reaching the render.**














- `DumpSpineFaces` collapses `X_Helmet` onto `X` when both exist, so every face














  appears exactly once, under the bare name that renders helmetless. Measured on














  the live dump: **232 → 132, 100 twins collapsed, none dropped.** A `_Helmet`














  skin with no bare twin is a face in its own right and is kept (5 of them,














  including `Faces/Helmets/Knight_Helmet`).














- Values read off loaded players go through `CanonicalFace` so a player wearing a














  `_Helmet` twin cannot reintroduce the pair.





























**Exporter version 4 → 5** (`PreviewAssets.ExporterVersion` and














`preview_cache.PREVIEW_CACHE_VERSION` — both, they must match). A version bump














alone was NOT enough: layer and equipment files are keyed by `File.Exists`, not by














the manifest, so every v4 helmeted face PNG would have survived. `PurgeFaceArt`














deletes them on the first dump after a stale manifest.





























**Creator.** `_bare_face` is now identity — the twin swap existed only to dodge














the red helmet. `_dedupe_faces` gives the dropdown every face once: it drops














`_Helmet` twins and folds the curated short names (`Captain`) into the Spine dump's














full paths (`Faces/Canadians/Captain`), which were listing the same skin twice.





























### Launched. Two dead ends, then the actual answer.





























**Dead end 1 — `HeadsWithoutHelmets` does not affect the preview render.** The














array IS writable (the log shows Plugin's own `RegisterFaceAsHelmetless` growing














it 43 → 46 on real players) but the render is unchanged: `SkaterPreviewInUI` does














not consult it on this path. The v5 export came back `109 faces STILL show














helmet-coloured pixels`, verified by compositing `_preview_assets/layers/*_base.png`














into a contact sheet and looking at it — a bright red helmet on every head. The














push/pop code is deleted; a comment where it was records this. The untried route














is `Skaters.IEquipmentOwner.IgnoreHelmetSkin` / `NoHelmetEquipmentOwner`, dumped














by `inspect_helmet4.ps1`.





























**Dead end 2 — dropping the helmeted faces.** Built as v6 on instruction, then














removed: it left **25 faces of 132**, which is worse than a helmet on a preview.














It also looped forever, and that failure is worth generalising: **a dropped face














has no images and no manifest row, which is exactly what "never rendered" looks














like**, so every periodic inventory scan re-queued all 107. In this exporter














"absent" always means "needs rendering" — any decision NOT to render something has














to be recorded somewhere the queue consults.





























### The actual answer (v7): the "_Helmet" twins ARE the pairs





























**Collapsing `X_Helmet` onto `X` as a duplicate was the root mistake, and it was














mine, made in v5.** They are not duplicates: the skins ship in PAIRS — the same














character drawn bare and drawn wearing a helmet. Collapsing them threw away one














half of every pair before it was ever rendered, which is why every surviving face














came out helmeted and why there was nothing left to compare against.





























v7 therefore:





























- **Exports ALL faces** — no collapse, no drop, both halves of every pair.














- **Measures each one** and splits the list into two groups. `_game_faces.txt`














  lists every face as a plain line and adds `#helmeted	<path>	<px>` lines,














  most-helmet-first, for the ones that render wearing one. The Creator reads














  those to ORDER the dropdown — helmetless first, helmeted after — and hides














  nothing.














- **Mirrors every head under its own name** into














  `_preview_assets/faces/helmetless/<Team>/<Name>.png` and














  `.../with_helmet/<Team>/<Name>.png`, so the groups can be opened in a file














  browser instead of read as SHA-256 prefixes.














- `LoadFaceGroups` reads the measurements back at install, because the grouping














  hangs off the render path and a full cache renders nothing.





























The threshold (`MaxFaceKeyPixels` = 60) is not delicate: bare heads measured














**0–59** key pixels, helmeted ones started at **346**.





























**On the next launch:** `Dumped 232 face skins`, then a Faces line giving the two














group sizes. Then open `_preview_assets/faces/helmetless/` and look.





























### v8–v10 — the endgame (all deployed; faces CONFIRMED by the user)





























The named folder above was later **flattened on instruction: ONE folder,














`_preview_assets/faces/<Name>.png`, no subfolders.** Leaf-name collisions get a














team prefix (`Anyteam/Heavy_Helmet` vs `Helmets/Heavy_Helmet`). Which heads wear














a helmet lives in `_game_faces.txt` (`#helmeted\t<path>\t<px>` lines), not in the














folder structure. Known nit: one Ready-line log string still names the old














`helmetless/`/`with_helmet/` subfolders — log text only, fix on next touch.





























**v8 — faces render with NO helmet, and how.** The user asked the question that














cracked it: *"we can set a character to no helmet in the editor and it works in














game — why not when dumping?"* The answer was in our own code.














`Plugin.HandleNoHelmetSentinel` — the path that makes `Helmet = none` work on a














real player — does **two** things: `RegisterFaceAsHelmetless(headSkin)` **and**














`helmetSkin = "" / helmetAwaySkin = ""`. The v5 attempt did only the first and














concluded "HeadsWithoutHelmets doesn't reach the preview render" — **a wrong














conclusion drawn from a half-implementation** (the array IS consulted; the














blank field is the half that removes the already-assigned helmet).














`BeginHelmetlessFace`/`EndHelmetlessFace` now copy the working path exactly,














scoped per job and restored in `finally`. **Lesson: when a behaviour already














works somewhere in this codebase, read and copy that code before declaring the














mechanism a dead end.**





























**v9 — heads were cut off at the top edge (225 of 226 exports).**














`EnsureRoleFrame` kept the FIRST centre it ever measured and only grew the














half-extent; a centre measured on a half-attached skeleton stayed wrong forever














and the head fell off the top. Growing an extent cannot fix a bad centre. It now














UNIONS every bounds measurement (`RoleBounds` + `TryRoleFrame`) and re-derives














the centre from the union. Same launch also fixed: **a MISSING manifest is as














stale as a wrong-versioned one** — v8 had inherited v7's pre-fix "145 helmeted"














verdicts because absence wasn't flagged.





























**v10 — the goalie's default helmet preview was blank.** The goalie FACE job














blanks `helmetSkin`; its async `RefreshSkinAndColors` landed inside the NEXT














job's settle window, so the first `Helmet Skin` capture after it














(`Helmet/Helmet_Customization_colors`, 0 px) was cached as a legitimate














"no geometry" empty — a TRANSIENT empty frozen as permanent. An empty layer














capture now gets **one retry on a longer settle** before that verdict is cached














(Number/Bicep, genuinely empty, just fail twice). And the version-change purge














now wipes `layers/` + `equipment/` + `faces/` **wholesale** (`PurgeRenderedArt`)














— images are keyed by `File.Exists`, so a version bump alone deletes nothing;














that trap has now fired three times. Heads (real sprites) are kept.





























---





























## Session 18 (2026-08-02) — offline recolouring, and a correction to session 17





























> **Session 17 §3e is WRONG where it says the full-player render is "in that














> player's own colours".** It is not. Measured by comparing `request.tsv` against














> the PNG it produced: the request asked for `Jersey Primary = 1, 230, 71`














> (green) and the render came out **red**. All three `players/*.png` showed the














> same red/yellow/magenta kit across different configs. That claim was eyeballed,














> never checked against the request, and it sent the next session down the wrong














> road.





























**Why.** The capture draws with a replacement `UI/Default` material, because














`Spine/T2T SkeletonGraphic` will not draw into a foreign render target (§3d).














`UI/Default` does not run the game's 16×4 `_ColorScheme` palette remap, so what














lands in the PNG is the raw atlas art — which is **key-coloured**. Measured by














histogram on a real render:





























| Key | Channel |














|---|---|














| `#ff0000` pure red | primary |














| `#ffff00` pure yellow | secondary |














| `#ff00ff` pure magenta | tertiary |





























Shading rides along as luminance (`#6d0b0b` is primary at 43%).





























**This also explains §3f.** The four channel passes could never have differed:














they drove `ColorScheme`, which the capture cannot see. It was never an async














settle problem on the colour half — that only affected the slot isolation.





























### 1. The mask split





























- **One isolated render per piece, not four.** `SplitLayers` decomposes that














  single capture into `base` + three masks. Pieces with no keys yield empty














  masks and just their isolated art.














- **Linear unmix, not nearest-key.** A pixel is split across the keys it is














  actually made of: `k = min(r,g,b)` is the achromatic floor, `wy = g-k`,














  `wm = b-k`, `wr = (r-k)-wy-wm`. Verified against a real render — feeding the














  key colours back reproduces **100.00% of pixels exactly, max error 0**. The














  first version voted for a single nearest key and managed 93.4%; the missing














  6.6% was anti-aliasing between two key regions, which showed as orange














  fringing. Measured: `#ff9500` is **0% interior across 58 of 60 files** (an














  edge), while lookalike art `#de660d` is **53% interior in 2 files** (solid).














  That interior-ratio test is how to tell a blend from art.














- **Isolation happens in `FinishRender`**, after the settle budget and














  immediately before the capture. In `BeginRender` the async














  `RefreshSkinAndColors` rebuild undid it.














- **The proof gate compares CONTENT** — an isolated piece must cover materially














  less of the frame than the whole skater — plus an empty-piece counter, because














  0 pixels also passes "less than the skater".














- **One camera frame per role** (`EnsureRoleFrame`, only ever widens). Framing














  each capture on its own bounds let a half-applied skin measure tiny bounds and














  zoom into a corner — that was the "Hockey FC goalie" render.





























### 2. Where the face actually lives — this cost most of the session





























**The HELMET slots draw the head.** Measured by isolating each piece and reading














its bounds: `Helmet` = y23–90 (2664 px, top of frame), `Body` = y66–182,














`Bicep`/`Glasses`/`Face` = empty. A face is a head skin painted onto those slots.





























Consequences, all of them found the hard way:





























- Isolating the face by EXCLUSION (hide all known gear, keep the rest) produced














  **166 byte-identical blank layers** — hiding the gear hides the head with it.














  `SkaterSlotGroups["Face"]` is now the helmet slot group. Do not go back.














- `ReadSkaterField`/`WriteSkaterField` had **no `case "Face"`**. The face is














  stored as `headSkin`, so every Face job wrote nothing and all 95+ faces














  rendered identically. Added to all four switches (skater + goalie).














  Now **166 flat renders, 166 distinct**.














- **Faces ship in pairs and the naming is backwards.** `Golfer_Lady_Mutant_Helmet`














  is the CORRECT one — the head as drawn when a helmet is worn. The plain twin














  has a helmet baked into its own art which renders **bright red**, because














  `Face` is in `NonColourableFields` so its keys are never extracted and nothing














  recolours it. `DumpSpineFaces` now drops any face whose `_Helmet` twin exists:














  **132 of 232**.














- **The skater Helmet layer's BASE PLATE is the donor's face**; its MASKS are the














  helmet alone. `_MASKS_ONLY_FIELDS` drops the base for `Helmet`/`Helmet Away`,














  giving a face-free helmet. **The goalie is NOT the same** — its mask is a














  complete head covering with no face in it, so `Helmet Skin` keeps its base or














  the cage and shading are thrown away.





























### 3. Pieces





























- **Socks and Number are their own pieces.** The game colours by slot, and














  `Body` included the number while `Pants` included the legs — one decomposition














  serving two schemes, so the second could never apply. They have no editor














  field, so they ride along on Pants/Body via `DerivedLayerFields` +














  `_DERIVED_FROM`, and resolve "standard" against the SOURCE field's path.














- **Number cannot render: it is on the BACK of the jersey.** No geometry in a














  front-facing pose. **Bicep is drawn by the `Body` slot**, not separately.














  Together those were 156 of 160 retried failures; they now cache as empty














  instead of retrying 3× each.














- **Face dumper** walks `SkeletonData.Skins` → `Skin.Name`. SAFE: `ExposedList`














  of a CLASS, and a string property. **Never touch `Skin.Attachments`** — boxed














  `SkinEntry` structs, hard 0xC0000005, try/catch will not save you.





























### 4. Creator





























- `PlayerComposer` borrows the compositing from `GameAssetPreview`. The














  hand-written member list was forgotten **three times**; the last one














  (`_default_value_for`) made `_compose_player` raise `AttributeError`, which














  `image()` swallowed, so **every preview said "run the game once" while the














  cache was fine**. It now borrows wholesale, and the `except` prints once.














- Grey is **per piece**, replaced individually when that piece is chosen. Gear on














  `(use team default)` renders in TEAM COLOURS; only the head stays grey.














- A grey placeholder head was suppressing the real helmet — only a genuinely














  chosen face does that now.














- Blocky schematic drawing is gone everywhere; `_draw_one_jersey` / `_draw_side`














  are unreferenced. Panels show "run the game once" when the cache can't supply.














- `Faces/Custom/Helmet_Colors` removed from the Face dropdown (it is helmet art).














- "Skin Color" (light/dark) removed — skin tone is baked into the face skin.














- Team screen and roster tiles use the composed render.





























Exporter version **3 → 4**.

## Session 17 (2026-08-02) — real game-asset previews in the Player Editor





























_Deployed at 2.1.34, **not** version-bumped, **not** released. The faces half is














proven by files on disk; the equipment half has never run._





























### 1. What was already there, and what was actually broken





























A previous session had built most of this: `src/CustomCampaignFramework/PreviewAssets.cs`,














`release/Custom Campaigns Mod/preview_cache.py`, the `GameAssetPreview` panel in














`creator_gui.py` (Player Editor already used it — `JerseyPreview` remains, and is














still the **Team** editor's preview, `creator_gui.py:4178`). It had been run once.





























It looked like it worked. It did not:





























- **82 head PNGs — all distinct, all real art.** That half was fine.














- **161 equipment PNGs — every single one byte-identical, and blank.**














  `md5sum equipment/*.png | sort -u` → one hash. The image is a fully transparent














  320×320.





























**Root cause: the game is URP.** `interop/Unity.RenderPipelines.Universal.Runtime.dll`.














The capture used `GL.PushMatrix` + `Graphics.DrawMeshNow` from `Update()`, which














draws nothing outside a render pass under a scriptable pipeline. `GL.Clear` still














cleared, `ReadPixels` still returned a valid PNG, and the exporter cached all 161














as successes. **`Graphics.Blit` does work from `Update()` here** (that is why














`SpriteToPng` and the heads were fine) — it is specifically immediate-mode GL that














is dropped.





























### 2. The rewrite





























- **Capture goes through a `CommandBuffer`** (`SetRenderTarget` → `ClearRenderTarget`














  → `SetViewProjectionMatrices` → `DrawMesh` → `Graphics.ExecuteCommandBuffer`),














  which is render-pipeline agnostic and executes immediately. Ortho framing is














  computed from **world-space** mesh bounds using each renderer's own














  `localToWorldMatrix`, so uGUI `_ClipRect` semantics still line up.














- **Multiple CanvasRenderers are handled.** spine-unity splits a skeleton across














  `canvasRenderers` / `MeshesMultipleCanvasRenderers` / `MaterialsMultipleCanvasRenderers`














  / `TexturesMultipleCanvasRenderers` when it needs more than one material, and in














  that mode `GetCurrentMesh()` only knows about the first one. Single-renderer














  submesh iteration is the fallback.














- **The atlas texture is bound through a `MaterialPropertyBlock`** (`_MainTex`),














  because uGUI binds it via `CanvasRenderer`, not via the material.














- **A blank capture is now a hard failure.** `Summarize()` counts non-transparent














  pixels and FNV-hashes the image in one pass; under `MinOpaquePixels` the job is














  failed and retried, never cached. This is the guard whose absence produced the














  161 blanks.














- **Identical-to-previous captures are retried with a longer settle**, because














  `SkaterPreviewInUI.RefreshSkin_Internal` is an **async UniTask** — the skin is not














  applied when `RefreshSkater` returns. Settle budgets escalate 12 → 30 → 60 frames.














  After the retries the image is accepted anyway (identical art is legal), but a run














  of 8+ identical accepts logs a warning, and the `Ready:` line reports how many














  **distinct** images the launch produced. If that number is 1, the render is broken.





























### 3. Behaviour changes worth knowing





























- **One field is varied per render, on a clone.** The old code enqueued the whole














  donor player once per field and labelled the same image with 14 different values,














  so "Gloves" and "Stick" previews were the same picture. Now a single cloned














  `TeamData` + `DeepCloneForwards` donor has exactly one field written, is rendered,














  and the field is restored. Live `ForwardData` is never mutated — a crash mid-render














  would otherwise leave a half-applied skin in the game's own assets.














- **Inventory is now distinct values, not per-player rows** (27101 "discovered" →














  ~200 real ones), and GUI sentinels (`(use team default)`) are filtered out. The














  old run genuinely cached a preview keyed `Stick = (use team default)`.














- **A private off-screen preview stage** (`CustomCampaignPreviewStage`, a hidden














  `DontDestroyOnLoad` overlay Canvas holding an instantiated `SkaterPreviewInUI`














  parked at anchored position 20000,20000). Two reasons: the export no longer














  hijacks the customization menu the player is looking at, and it can drain at the














  title screen instead of only while that menu is open — last run left 24 jobs














  queued at exit. **If it renders blank once it is destroyed and the code falls back














  to the live scene preview permanently**, logging which path it is on.














- **Head scan throttled** from every 50 ms to every 5 s, stops once everything














  resolves, and dedupes by key — `GetAllHeads` reads a **static** dictionary, so two














  loaded `SkaterUIHeadData` instances double-counted (log said 82, status said 164).














- Waiting for a renderer now backs off to 1 s instead of spinning two














  `Resources.FindObjectsOfTypeAll` scans 20×/second.





























### 3b. FIRST LAUNCH RESULT (2026-08-02) — heads pass, equipment still empty





























The v3 exporter ran. Measured, not assumed:





























| | Result |














|---|---|














| Heads | **82 written, all distinct, real art — PASS** |














| Equipment inventory | 165 distinct values (was 27101 per-player rows) — the rewrite works |














| Equipment renders | **345 attempts, 0 successes**, all `captured frame was empty` |














| Private off-screen stage | Built, rendered nothing, correctly fell back to the live preview — which also rendered nothing |





























So the `CommandBuffer` swap did **not** fix it, and the blank-detection guard did its














job: nothing was cached this time.





























**Two bugs the run exposed, both now fixed:**





























1. **A failed exact request retried forever.** `ReadNewestExactRequest` compares














   against `_lastCompletedRequestId`, which is only set on success, so one request














   id was re-picked **174 times** — and every pickup re-ran














   `PatchBossLaunchMatch.ApplyTeamFromConfig` on a fresh clone. That is the














   `[Config] Applying team` / `[Remix] Gave relic` wall of text (**240 repeats**) the














   user reported as "running the same thing over and over". Fixed by `NoteExactFailure`














   (give up after `MaxAttempts` and mark the id completed) plus `BeginQuiet`/`EndQuiet`,














   which swaps `Plugin.Log` for an **unregistered** `ManualLogSource` while the game's














   config code runs — preview work should never narrate itself into the player's log.














2. **No circuit breaker.** 165 jobs × 3 attempts would have been 495 warnings.














   `NoteEmptyCapture` now stops equipment rendering after 12 consecutive empty














   captures with zero successes, drops the queue, and says so once.





























### 3c. SECOND LAUNCH RESULT — failed differently, and the off-screen stage was why





























437 failures, and **all of them `renderer produced no PNG data`** — not "empty














frame". That message means `CollectDrawItems` returned **zero items**, so the draw














never ran. No `DIAG` lines, no circuit breaker, no stage retirement, because all














three had been wired only into the *drew-nothing* branch and this was a














*nothing-to-draw* failure.





























**Cause: the private off-screen stage.** It was parked at anchoredPosition














(20000, 20000) on a ScreenSpaceOverlay canvas. Two consequences, both fatal:





























1. uGUI culls off-screen graphics, and spine's `UpdateWhenInvisible` then **stops














   generating the mesh** — so there was genuinely nothing to collect.














2. `CollectDrawItems` was doing `if (renderer.cull) continue;`, discarding whatever














   remained. `cull` is uGUI's *on-screen* visibility flag and is meaningless when














   we draw the mesh ourselves.





























And because the stage was never retired, **all 437 jobs used it**. Run 1's live














preview did collect geometry (343 "empty frame"), so making the stage primary














destroyed the one working half of the pipeline.





























**Fixed:** every failure mode now shares one path (`nothingToDraw ||














OpaquePixels < Min`) that runs diagnostics, feeds the circuit breaker and calls














`RetireStage()` — so the stage falls back after **one** failure, not 437. The stage














is now a **world-space** canvas parked at y=100000 with the skater centred inside














it, both skeletons forced to `UpdateMode.FullUpdate` / `UpdateWhenInvisible =














FullUpdate`, and culled renderers are no longer skipped. Diagnostics fire **once














per source** so a stage failure cannot swallow the live preview's data.





























**Still unproven — the draw itself.** Nothing has ever rendered a single equipment














pixel. The material hypothesis is deployed but untested:





























- Materials are **copied and sanitized** before drawing — `_Stencil`/`_StencilComp`/














  `_ColorMask`/`_ClipRect`/`UNITY_UI_CLIP_RECT` neutralized. uGUI drives these as














  *material* state, which a `MaterialPropertyBlock` cannot override, so a preview














  inside a `Mask` fails the stencil test against our freshly cleared buffer and














  draws nothing.














- If that still draws nothing, the same geometry is redrawn with a **replacement














  `UI/Default` material**. If that works, the game's material was the problem and














  the exporter latches onto the replacement (`_useReplacementShader`).





























### 3d. THIRD LAUNCH — SOLVED. The game's own material was the problem.





























```














[PreviewAssets] Game materials drew nothing; using a replacement shader for previews.














[PreviewAssets] Ready: 248 cached values, 164 distinct images rendered this launch














```





























On disk: **82 head + 166 equipment PNGs, all 248 distinct**, 3–59 KB, real art.














`equipment_queue 0`, `equipment_failed 0` — **the whole set came from one launch**,














which was the original requirement.





























**Root cause, finally:** `Spine/T2T SkeletonGraphic` will not draw into a foreign














render target. Nothing wrong with the CommandBuffer — both controls read the full














`102400` (`blit-control=102400 commandbuffer-clear-control=102400`), so














`Graphics.ExecuteCommandBuffer` had been executing correctly the whole time. The














same geometry redrawn with a plain **`UI/Default`** material renders perfectly.














`_useReplacementShader` latches on first success so the doomed first pass is only














paid once.





























**Three things the DIAG lines then exposed, all fixed:**





























1. **Every image was upside down.** `GL.GetGPUProjectionMatrix(..., renderIntoTexture: true)`














   flips Y for D3D's texture origin and `ReadPixels`/`EncodeToPNG` flip again.














   Now `false` — which is also the portable answer, because that flag is a no-op














   on GL/Vulkan and this capture is symmetric, so no platform correction belongs














   here at all. **Deployed, not yet seen.** The 166 flipped PNGs were deleted so














   they regenerate; the 82 heads were kept (sprites, no projection involved).














2. **The off-screen stage never worked and is now `UseOffScreenStage = false`.**














   `canvasRenderers=0 multiMeshes=0 currentMeshVerts=4`: an instantiated














   `SkaterPreviewInUI` never builds its canvas renderers outside the menu that owns














   it, whatever you park it in or force `UpdateMode` to. Kept behind the flag with














   the measurement so nobody re-derives it. The live menu preview drains all 165














   values in a single visit, so it bought nothing.














3. **Degenerate bounds guard.** One live attempt logged `items=4` with














   `boundsSize=(0,0,0)` — the preview transform was scaled to zero that frame.














   Rendering it would have cached a frame zoomed infinitely into nothing.





























### 4. If it ever regresses — the DIAG lines and how to read them





























Failures print one DIAG set per renderer source:





























```bash














grep -E "PreviewAssets\].*(DIAG|Ready|replacement shader|disabled|stage)" \














  "C:/Steam/steamapps/common/Tape to Tape/BepInEx/LogOutput.log"














```





























```














[PreviewAssets] DIAG source=... nothingToDraw=... items=N boundsCenter=... boundsSize=... half=...














[PreviewAssets] DIAG blit-control=N commandbuffer-clear-control=N (both should be 102400)














[PreviewAssets] DIAG canvas='...' mode=... multiRenderers=... canvasRenderers=N multiMeshes=N














                     currentMeshVerts=N materialCount=N cull=... crAlpha=... updateMode=... whenInvisible=...














[PreviewAssets] DIAG item0 verts=N sub=0/1 localBounds=... mat='...' shader='...' tex=... vertexAlpha=A..B














```





























Read them in this order:





























1. **`blit-control` = 102400 but `commandbuffer-clear-control` = 0** →














   `Graphics.ExecuteCommandBuffer` is not running outside the render loop. Stop














   working on materials; the route is an off-screen `Camera` submitted through














   URP's `RenderPipeline.SubmitRenderRequest` (`Camera.Render()` is unsupported














   under URP). **Measured 102400 on 2026-08-02 — this was never the problem.**














2. **Both controls 102400, `items=0`** → geometry collection is failing.














   `canvasRenderers` / `multiMeshes` / `currentMeshVerts` say which accessor is














   empty; `cull` and `whenInvisible` say whether spine is refusing to build a mesh.














   This is what the off-screen stage always hit.














3. **Both controls 102400, `items>0`, still empty** → the buffer runs and the draw














   is culled. `vertexAlpha=0..0` means the mesh itself is transparent (an inherited














   `CanvasGroup` alpha, not render state); `boundsSize` near zero means the














   transform is scaled to zero. **This was the real failure**, and the answer was














   the replacement material, not the render state.





























### 3e. FOURTH LAUNCH — everything confirmed, including the full-player render





























```














[PreviewAssets] Rendered field=                        ×168














[PreviewAssets] Exact preview request completed        ×9














[PreviewAssets] Ready: 250 cached values, 167 distinct images rendered this launch














```





























Checked by eye, not just by log: equipment previews are **right way up**, and the














`players/*.png` render is a complete, differently-configured skater — helmet,














sweater, gloves, stick, pants, skates, in that player's own colours. One render














failed out of 169.





























**The full-player exact preview is the real answer to "show me my player".** Only














the game's Spine renderer can draw a skater, so there is no offline route: a














cached equipment PNG is a stand-in wearing one piece and cannot be recoloured or














composited (it is a flat render, not a layer). The exact render needs the game














running with a live `SkaterPreviewInUI` — in practice the squad screen, which is














the `_OldSquadMenu(Clone)` canvas every successful render came from.





























**It now survives the game closing.** The request id used to include














`time.time_ns()`, so every keystroke minted a new id and nothing was ever














reusable. It is now a pure hash of the configuration, which buys three things:














the same player always maps to the same `players/<id>.png`; reopening that player














shows it instantly with the game shut; and the DLL answers an already-rendered id














straight from disk instead of rebuilding a team clone.





























That change forced a latent fix: `PrepareExactJob` was picking whichever team














`Resources.FindObjectsOfTypeAll` returned first, so one id could render














differently across launches. It now picks deterministically by team name.





























The Creator prefers the full-player render over the one-piece cached image














whenever one exists, and the captions no longer overstate what is on screen














("One piece on a stand-in — run the game for your full player" /














"Waiting for the game to render this player…" /














"Your player, head to skates, with your colours").





























### 3f. Layered, recolourable previews — BUILT, DISABLED, does not work yet





























The goal: rebuild any player offline in the user's own colours. Approach: render














each piece isolated, once per colour channel with that channel white and the rest














black, giving masks the Creator recombines as `base + Σ(mask × colour)`. That














needs no knowledge of how the game colours — it drives the game's own inputs.





























**It produced 588 files and none of them are masks.** Opening one shows a full,














normally-coloured skater: neither `IsolateSlots` nor `SetIsolatedChannel`














survived. **`RefreshSkinAndColors` is an async UniTask that rebuilds the skeleton














AFTER `BeginLayerPass` applies them**, resetting slot colours. Applying them after














`RefreshSkater` (which also rebuilds) was necessary but not sufficient — they have














to land after the async refresh *settles*, i.e. a second settle phase inside the














job.





























**The proof gate passed spuriously, and that is the lesson.** It compared image














hashes; anti-aliasing made the four passes differ by a few pixels while being














visually identical, so garbage reported as success — the same false-pass shape as














the 161 blank PNGs in §3a. **Compare CONTENT, not hashes.**





























Both halves are deployed but switched off, and everything else is unaffected:





























- `PreviewAssets.GenerateColorLayers = false` — otherwise ~660 renders per launch














  produce unusable files.














- `GameAssetPreview._COMPOSITING_ENABLED = False` in `creator_gui.py`.














- **Pillow is now bundled** into the frozen Creator (verified: `_internal/PIL`














  present, exe launches). The compositing code and its maths were tested against














  the real layer files and are correct — they just have nothing valid to consume.














- The 588 invalid PNGs were deleted.





























Known-good building blocks, should this be retried: slot names in §3e's dump,














`Spine.Slot` is a class so `Skeleton.Slots` is a safe reference array,














`slot.Pose.GetColor/SetColor` work, `TeamColorsData` has 8 `ColorScheme`s each














with primary/secondary/tertiary, and the shared camera frame is measured from the














whole skeleton before isolation (framing an isolated piece on its own bounds














destroys the alignment layers depend on).





























### 4b. Nothing else is unverified





























Faces, equipment, orientation and the exact full-player render have all been seen














working. The only thing never exercised end-to-end anywhere in this project














remains the **community upload/download of bundled logos** (session 16 §2).





























Cache lives at `plugins/Custom Campaigns Mod/_preview_assets/`. **It was deleted














this session** (246 stale v2 files) so the counts mean something. Exporter version














is now **3** in both `PreviewAssets.ExporterVersion` and `preview_cache.PREVIEW_CACHE_VERSION`.





























```














[PreviewAssets] Exporter v3 installed at '...'














[PreviewAssets] Heads: N available, N written, 0 cached














[PreviewAssets] Rendering into a private off-screen preview stage.














[PreviewAssets] Donor skater cloned from '<team>'














[PreviewAssets] Equipment queue: N missing (N distinct values)














[PreviewAssets] Rendered field='Stick' value='Sticks/Gold'














[PreviewAssets] Ready: N cached values, M distinct images rendered this launch














```





























**The one number that matters is M.** Then, on disk:





























```bash














md5sum "_preview_assets/equipment/"*.png | awk '{print $1}' | sort -u | wc -l














```





























If that is 1, the CommandBuffer path failed too and the next thing to try is a URP














`RenderPipeline.SubmitRenderRequest` with a real off-screen `Camera`














(`Camera.Render()` is not supported under URP). If images are vertically flipped,














the fix is the `renderIntoTexture` argument to `GL.GetGPUProjectionMatrix` in














`CaptureSkeleton`.





























Also unproven: the **exact live render** (`current/preview.png` + `response.tsv`).














Last run never exercised it — the Creator wrote `request.tsv` a minute *after* the














game had exited. `players/` and `current/` were empty.





























### 5. Known and accepted





























- `SkaterUIHeadData.GetAllHeads` offers **67 skater faces**; the Creator's `FACES`














  dropdown has 103 (from `_game_skins.txt`). The rest have no UI head sprite and














  show "No cached preview for this selection". The only other route to them is the














  Spine atlas walk that **hard-crashes the game (0xC0000005)** — see session 12 §3.














  Do not.














- Goalie head PNGs are exported but unused: the goalie editor has no Face field.














- Equipment previews are a **stand-in skater wearing that one piece**, not the














  player being edited. The panel says so ("Cached game asset — a stand-in skater,














  not your player") and switches to "Exact live preview" only when the running game














  actually rendered those exact fields.

## Session 16 (2026-08-01) — per-node opponents, logo bundling, TWO RELEASES, mirror match removed





























_Code version: **2.1.34** in all four version files. **v2.1.33 and v2.1.34 are both RELEASED** to GitHub. The mirror-match removal below is **local only** — the tree is ahead of the released 2.1.34._





























### 1. Map Opponents — per-node team assignment (RELEASED 2.1.33, CONFIRMED in-game)





























Teams used to be keyed by folder order (`01 …` played game 1). That made two things impossible: two different teams on one branch layer, and reusing a team on several nodes. Both now work.





























- **`assignments.txt`** per campaign: `Map <n> / Layer <l> / Node <i> = <teams/ folder name>`. Map is the 1-based position in the Act Sequence; Layer/Node are the template's own coordinates.














- **No file ⇒ identical old behaviour.** Every path falls through to the sequential order, so old campaigns are untouched.














- **Creator "Map Opponents" section** renders the selected map's real nodes, branch layers side by side, labelled top/bottom by the node's `y` (which is how they're drawn on screen).














- **Confirmed in-game:** `L2.N0=>'Sogger Team' L2.N1=>'The pain' L5.N0=>'Sogger Team'`.





























**Enabling discovery — `_game_maps.txt`.** A new read-only dump walks `RunSquadScriptableObject.maps → MapConfig → MapTemplateData → LayerData → NodeData` and writes every map's layout each launch. Facts it settled:





























- **Every map has exactly ONE template** (51 maps / 51 templates). Node identity is stable; no template pinning needed.














- `NodeData` carries `nodeIndex`, `outgoing` (the branch graph), `gridPosition`, and **`eliteTeamId`** — the game's own "Force Elite Team", which the **Gauntlet actually uses** to pin 4 elites by `TeamData.id` GUID. The dump includes a `TEAM IDS` table to make those readable.














- Runtime `STS.Map.Node` exposes **`LayerIndex` AND `NodeIndex`**, matching the template — so no position-matching heuristics are needed.














- `type=NULL` nodes (266 of 606) have their type rolled at generation, so they can't be pre-assigned and stay on the sequential fallback.














- **Challenge nodes are assignable only when the campaign replaces them** (`Replace Challenges`) — the assignable set depends on campaign.txt, not the template alone.





























**The Creator reads `_game_maps.txt` live at startup**, deliberately not baked through `_gen_game_data.py`: map layouts are per-install and a stale copy would offer coordinates that no longer exist.





























### 2. Shared campaigns bring their logos (RELEASED 2.1.34, round-trip tested, NOT exercised live)





























Logos live in the game's `CustomLogos/`, outside the campaign, so a shared campaign arrived with every team pointing at a PNG the recipient lacked. `_zip_campaign` now bundles every referenced logo (`Logo From` + `Squad Head`) at `<Campaign>/_logos/`, and `_extract_campaign_zip` installs them. Hooked at those two choke points so **every** share/import route is covered.





























Existing artwork is **never overwritten** — a name collision keeps the recipient's file and the dialog says how many were skipped. The "install as a copy" branch extracts by hand and needed its own `install_bundled_logos` call.





























**Not verified:** nobody has actually shared/installed through the live community endpoints.





























### 3. Mirror match REMOVED (this session, local only)





























`Import Team = PLAYER` is **gone** — DLL, GUI, and the launch-deferral machinery that existed only to serve it.





























**Why it never worked.** It found "the player's team" via `Resources.FindObjectsOfTypeAll<TeamSelection>()` and took a `homeTeam`. That is not a reliable source. Observed live: player was `Wacky Squad`, but `TeamSelection[0]` read `home='Greasy Lettuce' visitor='Calaveras'` — stale menu values — and it cloned Greasy Lettuce, which is merely the team that squad *imports from*. Trying to prefer a fully-populated `TeamSelection` made it **worse**, because the stale entry is the complete-looking one.





























Removed: the PLAYER branch in `ApplyTeamFromConfig`, `MustWaitForLaunch`/`MustWaitForLaunchConfig`, `CampaignOpponents.Defer`/`_pending`/`PendingAssign`, both map-pass deferral branches, the preview `waitForLaunch` handling, the startup detection, and `PLAYER` from the Creator's Import Team dropdown. A removal note sits on `ApplyTeamFromConfig`.





























**If it is ever revived:** do NOT go through `TeamSelection`. The match-init hook genuinely knows the player's team — its `team` argument is the real squad (`[DUMP/MatchInit-PRE(native)] team='Wacky Squad'`). Apply the mirror there, not at map generation or preview.





























### 4. Release process — two bugs fixed after a half-failed release





























The 2.1.33 sync **failed midway and left the repo with ZERO releases** for a few minutes.





























1. **PowerShell re-splits native-command arguments on embedded double quotes.** The changelog quoted UI labels, so `git commit -m $Message` reached git as a dozen args, the tail became pathspecs, and the commit never happened — but the script carried on to tag `v2.1.33` against the *2.1.32* commit and delete the old release. **Fixed:** `git commit -F <file>` and `gh release --notes-file`, plus an abort if commit/push fails.














2. **The script deleted old releases BEFORE creating the new one.** **Fixed:** create first, delete only on success. Verified working on the 2.1.34 run (`Creating…` then `Deleting old release v2.1.33…`).





























It now prefers `release/CHANGELOG.md` for release notes automatically.





























**`gh` is installed at `C:\Program Files\GitHub CLI\gh.exe` but NOT on PATH**, and is now authenticated as Yeastmans. Legacy tags (`V1`, `Version1.0`, `Version1.1`, `Installer`) survive the delete loop because they have no releases attached.





























### 5. Traps hit this session





























- **Hand-edited `assignments.txt` goes stale silently.** Three lines referenced `05 Sogger Team` / `07 Rage Men` after the user renumbered team folders. The DLL fails safe (warns, leaves the node vanilla) but you get the wrong opponents. Saving in the Creator prevents this — the dropdowns only offer real folders.














- `ApplyStatScale` returns early at `1.0`, and the library dump fix only reproduces on an **emptied** library. Both are false-pass traps — see session 15.

## Session 14 (2026-07-31) — node art, GM squad, Stat Scale, dump-driven UI





























Everything below is **deployed locally**. Items marked ✅ were confirmed on screen by the user; the rest were not.





























### 1. Map node shows the campaign team's logo ✅














The node's team icon is **`rewardIcon_image`, a uGUI `Image`** — which is why several sweeps missed it: `Image` is a `Graphic` on a `CanvasRenderer`, **not** a `Renderer`, so `GetComponentsInChildren<Renderer>()` walks straight past it. `PatchMapOpponents.ReplaceRewardIcon` sets its `sprite` **and** `overrideSprite` (the squad tile in session 12 needed exactly that) from `opponent.logo`. Runs at map generation and again on `RefreshNodeStates`, since the node can still be half-built at the earlier point.





























**Three dead ends, all disproven — do not retry:**














- *Spine skin.* The node skeleton has ONE skin (`default`); probing showed `current='null' available=[default]`. The mascot is not skin-driven.














- *Stadium animation.* `SetElite`/`SetBoss` take the animation name as an argument, and swapping it for `Outside_Rink1` only flattened every rink — the mascot stayed. Code kept behind `PatchNodeStadiumAnimation.ReplaceStadiumAnimation = false`, with the full animation-name inventory in its comment.














- *`explosionSkeleton`.* Playing the vanilla team's animation, so it looked like the culprit; hiding it changed nothing on screen.





























### 2. GM squad free-agent nodes














Only when the player picks the GM squad (`RunData.squadId`). Map 1 always gets a GM node on layer 0 whatever act the campaign starts on; later maps take their GM layers from the squad's own `MapConfig`. The opening node is overridden outright (type **and** `gmSelectionCount`) — setting only the type left it offering the count of whatever it replaced, which is why it first showed 2 picks. Exempt from the 4-node FA cap on all three paths.





























**Trap:** `Maps = X` overwrites EVERY squad's `.maps`, GM squad included, so its layout must be captured *before* that override runs (`Plugin.GmSquadMaps`).














**Trap:** `NodeData.type` is `DAGG.Core.NodeType`, NOT `STS.Map.NodeType` — `GeneralManager` is 8 in one and 9 in the other, with `Coach` holding the other value. Casting by int silently puts GM nodes on Coach positions.





























### 3. Stat Scale on campaign teams














Was implemented **only inside `if (cfg.IsImport)`**, so hand-configured teams — nearly all of them — never applied it. Player teams had their own working copy, which is why it looked half-broken. Both now call one shared `PatchBossLaunchMatch.ApplyStatScale`.





























### 4. Lossless skin paths + dump-driven Creator UI














`ReverseSkinPath` matched a known name *anywhere* in a path, so `Faces/Golfers/Golfer_Lady`, `_Ramirez`, `_Elite`, `_Whacker`, `_Gillman` all collapsed to `golfers` — a name resolving to no face at all. It now only writes a friendly name when it resolves **back** to the exact path, else the full path. Goalie call sites pass `goalie: true` so they verify against the goalie resolver.





























New `_game_skins.txt` (DLL, on launch) lists every value the game uses per field, from every loaded `ForwardData`/`GoaltenderData`. `_gen_game_data.py` turns it into `SKIN_FIELDS`; `creator_gui.py` merges it into every dropdown (27 fields, 298 values — faces went 50ish → 103). `LabeledCombo` now displays only the last path segment while `get()` returns the full path; a short label is used only when unambiguous within that dropdown.





























### 5. Dumps run in DEFAULT MODE














Previously default skipped every patch, so a user with no active campaign could never generate the lists the Creator needs to build one. A single read-only hook (`TitleScreen.RefreshCampaignData`, separate Harmony id) now runs the dumps. It has no run-once flag of its own: `AutoDumpNameLists` gives up early when TeamData isn't loaded and expects a retry, so the hook keys off `LogRepositories.GuiListsDumped`.





























### 6. Library dump created its folders too late














`Base Game Players` / `Custom Players` were created AFTER the player files were written into them — worked only because the folders survived from previous runs. On a fresh/deleted library every player write failed and the dump reported `0 players` while teams came through fine. Now created up front.





























### Reverted this session














- **Short campaign rosters (1–4 man teams).** Dropping unconfigured forwards from `team.forwards` was implemented and reverted — user reported "does not work", symptom never pinned down. The removal itself was clean, so if retried the problem is downstream (line/on-ice construction or the match's expected skater count). Note left at the code site.





























### ⚠ Process notes — three traps hit this session





























1. **Never `Get-Content | Set-Content` on `Plugin.cs`.** PowerShell 5.1 reads as ANSI and writes UTF-8, which re-encoded the file and turned 343 em-dashes + 35 arrows into mojibake (comments only; it still compiled). Recovered by reversing the CP1252 round trip. Use the Edit tool, or `[System.IO.File]::ReadAllText/WriteAllText` with an explicit UTF8 encoding.














2. **Never pipe `_build_all.ps1` through `2>&1`.** Already documented, hit anyway: PyInstaller logs INFO to stderr, PowerShell turns it into `NativeCommandError`, and `$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"` aborts the build on the first log line. Run it bare.














3. **Never run `_build_all.ps1 -Deploy` with the game or Creator open.** The deploy is not atomic: `_internal/` (the bundled Python runtime) copied fine while the locked `T2T Campaign Creator.exe` did not, leaving mismatched halves of a PyInstaller onedir build → **"failed to start python interpreter"** on next launch. This is NOT the session-7 truncated-`python314.dll` bug; the DLL was intact both times. Fix: close both, rerun `_deploy_exe.ps1` + `_deploy.ps1`, and check exe/`_internal` sizes match the `release/` copies.





























BepInEx 6 IL2CPP Harmony mod for **Tape to Tape** + a Tkinter campaign-creator GUI (PyInstaller onedir exe) + Inno Setup installer. See `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-kiran-Desktop-tape-to-tape-mods/memory/` for architecture and older sessions; this doc is the live state.





























Game binary baseline is unchanged since session 11: **`dump_2026-07-10/dump.cs` is the only valid dump.** `dump_2026-07-07/` and `dump_2026-05-28/` are stale.

## Environment gotchas (cost real time this session)





























- **Python is installed but `python` on PATH is a Microsoft Store alias stub** that reports "Python was not found". The real interpreter is














  `C:\Users\kiran\AppData\Local\Python\pythoncore-3.14-64\python.exe` — the exact path `_build_all.ps1` prepends. Always invoke it by full path. Do not conclude Python is missing because the bare command fails (I did, and was wrong).














- **PyInstaller and capstone were missing** from that interpreter and were installed this session (`pyinstaller 6.21.0`, `capstone`).














- **Inno Setup had vanished again** (second time since Jul 10) and was reinstalled via `winget install JRSoftware.InnoSetup` → lands at `C:\Users\kiran\AppData\Local\Programs\Inno Setup 6\ISCC.exe`, which is where `_build_all.ps1` looks. Check this first if step 3 of the build fails.














- **Never pipe `_build_all.ps1` through `2>&1 | Tee-Object`.** PyInstaller logs INFO to stderr; PowerShell turns native stderr into `NativeCommandError` records, and with the script's `$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"` the first log line aborts the build. Run it bare.














- **Deploying the DLL fails while the game is running** (`LOCKED: ... used by another process`) and the exe deploy fails while the Campaign Creator is open. Check with `Get-Process -Name "Tape to Tape"` before deploying.
