## Session 12 (2026-07-31) — all DEPLOYED locally, NOT released





























### 1. Bench duplicate ("Stu Stumpl" / "Stu Stumple") — FIXED, verified by tests














The game renamed that bench player between versions. `draft_pool/` filenames are the match key the DLL uses (`DraftPoolConfigs[filename]` → matched against the in-game player name), so the rename left the old file inert while the seeding loop created a second one. Users saw a duplicate row, and edits to the old file silently stopped applying.





























- `_LEGACY_BENCH_FILES = {"Stu Stumpl": "Stu Stumple"}` + `_migrate_legacy_bench_files()` in `creator_gui.py` (`CampaignEditor`), run at the top of `_refresh_draft_list`. Add future game-side renames to that dict.














- Rules: no canonical file → plain rename; both present and identical, or canonical is an untouched copy of the library default → legacy content wins; both edited → keep canonical, park legacy as `.txt.bak` (invisible to GUI and DLL, both of which only read `*.txt`).














- Verified against real fixtures — all five branches pass (scratchpad test, not kept).














- Also: bench label count is now derived from `len(_DEFAULT_FREE_AGENTS)` (it said "7" when the list had grown to 9), and `_remove_draft_player` now uses `_draft_filename()` instead of the display text (dead code today — no Remove button — but it was wrong).














- Data cleaned in 4 campaigns (Example Campaign in release/, installer/linux_install/, deployed, plus Testing STUFF).





























### 2. Custom logos — SOLVED after months open














**Two chained bugs.**





























1. The game registers every PNG in `<persistentDataPath>/CustomLogos/` into `TeamAssetsRepositoryScriptableObject` as **`custom_<file name>.png`** (e.g. `custom_Canucks (25-26).png`). The mod looked them up by bare name, missed, and `GetLogo` silently returns its `defaultLogo` field — the `Teams_TapetoTape` sprite seen in every log.














2. Because teams then held that fallback sprite, `DumpTeamLogosToCustomLogos` **exported it back out** as `<team>.png` — 34 byte-identical copies of the house logo, which then shadowed the user's real artwork. It skips existing files, so they persisted.





























Fixes: `LogoIdCandidates(id)` returns `[id, "custom_" + bare + ".png"]` and `GetNativeLogo` accepts a sprite only when its name matches the candidate asked for; `IsFallbackLogo(sprite)` (name contains `TapetoTape`) now guards the dump. The 34 junk PNGs were moved to `CustomLogos/_placeholder_backup/`.





























**Confirmed working in-game** — the user's screenshot shows a Blackhawks crest on a jersey, matching `Applied CUSTOM logo 'CHI 3' (tex=500x500)`.





























Note: `Resources.FindObjectsOfTypeAll<Rogue.Relic>()`-style enumeration is used by the dumps; the real pack is installed under abbreviation names (`VAN 1`–`VAN 8`, `ANA 1`–`ANA 4`, 256 PNGs). `NHL 2026 RUN`'s 32 teams still have `Logo From = <Nickname> (25-26)` pointing at the now-quarantined placeholders — **the user is repointing these by hand in the GUI** (they declined a bulk rewrite). Every team's `Abbreviation` is correct, so `<ABBR> 1` is a valid bulk mapping if they ever want it. `01 Panthers (25-26)` has `Abbreviation = FLA` but `Logo From = Lightning (25-26)` — mismatched independently of this bug.





























### 3. Squad Head — semantics corrected, faces NOT possible














User: *"it should not [apply a face to a player], it should just be the tile"*, then *"do the logo's and currently working ones as the icon"*.





























- **Removed** the `startingTeam.forwards[keyPlayer].headSkin` write. It was a leftover from an older menu that rendered the tile from the key player's Spine skin; with the Jul-2026 menu its only effect was silently changing a skater's face.














- Tile head = `RunSquadScriptableObject.m_SmallTeamIcon` (offset `0x28`), drawn by `MetaTeamItem.m_KeyPlayerHead` — a plain uGUI `Image` set via **`overrideSprite`** (its `.sprite` stays `'00'` on every squad; probe `overrideSprite`, not `sprite`). Not Spine, so a runtime-created Sprite works here.














- `ResolveTileIcon` resolves: atlas region name → loaded sprite name → PNG in `CustomLogos/`, returning null (keeping the template icon) rather than blanking the tile.














- GUI dropdown now offers the **14 vanilla squad icons** (`Default Defense Speed Trio Butt Ref Slapshot Stats OldSchool Violence Tchell gm Solo Gauntlet` — read off a runtime probe) **+ `get_custom_logo_names()`**, not `FACES`.





























**⚠ Faces cannot resolve, and the obvious fix crashes the game.** A face like `Mathieu` is a Spine SKIN name (`Faces/Toronto/Mathieu`); no atlas region is named for it — a substring scan of all 10 loaded atlases (incl. the 2606-region `Player_Sprite_Atlas`) found nothing containing `Mathieu`. Walking `Skin.Attachments` to read each `RegionAttachment.Path` **crashed the game with 0xC0000005** at squad-clone time: `Skin.Attachments` is an Il2Cpp `ICollection` of non-blittable `SkinEntry` structs, and reading the boxed entries via IL2CPP reflection kills the process — it does not throw, so try/catch does not save you. That code is removed, with a warning comment at `PatchChooseMetaUI.FaceToSprite`. **Do not retry it that way.** If faces are ever wanted, the plausible route is extracting head art to PNGs during a match (skeleton live) and reusing them on the menu.





























### 4. Reward-pool exclusions freezing runs at game end — FIXED














Symptom: `NullReferenceException` at game end, run would not proceed, after `[RewardPool] Relic prefilter: added 282 exclusions`.





























Cause: the campaign excluded almost the entire pool; the game's own category/already-owned filters run on top and empty it, and its code NREs on the empty result.





























- `PatchFilterRelicRewards.Prefix` now takes `numberOfRelics`, counts the real pool, and stops adding exclusions once fewer than `numberOfRelics + 6` would remain, warning how many it ignored.














- **Counting matters:** `GetAllRelics()` returns an interop `IReadOnlyList` with no `Count` that will not cast to `IEnumerable` — it counted **0**, which made the budget `int.MaxValue` and silently disabled the guard (`pool 0` in the log). Now it sums the repository's category lists (`offensiveRelics`, `defensiveRelics`, … `coachRelics`), which are plain Il2Cpp Lists with a working `Count`. Real total: **61**.














- If the pool size still can't be determined it now **skips exclusions and warns**, rather than applying them blind.














- Same guard added to `PatchFilterTalentRewards.Prefix` via `TalentRepository.talents`.














- **Postfix consistency bug fixed:** the postfix re-applied the *full* exclusion set after the prefix had deliberately skipped some, cutting results 3 → 1. It now strips only `PatchFilterRelicRewards.AppliedExclusions` — the ids the prefix actually honoured.





























**⚠ User's correction (2026-07-31), not yet investigated:** *"I think there are different pools depending on the node."* This is very likely right and it undercuts the "61 is the total" reading above — `GetRandomRelics(relicCategory, …, matchAnyCategory)` is category-scoped, so 61 is probably the eligible set for **that call / that node type**, not a global catalogue. Before acting on the editor-list idea below, log `relicCategory` + the resulting pool size per node type (elite / boss / challenge / GM) and find out how the pool really varies. The exclusion guard is unaffected — it sizes whatever pool the current call sees — but any "show only real candidates" UI would need to be per-category, not one flat list.





























**Open, user's decision pending:** the reward-pool editor lists ~325 relics because `RELICS.txt` is built from `Resources.FindObjectsOfTypeAll<Rogue.Relic>()` (every loaded Relic object: upgrades, clones, instances). Only **61** were eligible in the one call observed. So the user excluded 293 entries, most meaningless, while unknowingly removing 51 of the 61 that mattered. Offered to make the picker list only real candidates (needs a DLL dump change + `_gen_game_data.py` regen + revisiting existing exclusion lists); **user has not answered yet.**

## Session 13 (2026-07-31) — map-generation opponents. DEPLOYED, NOT PLAYED YET





























The session-12 "next task" is implemented. **Nothing below has been seen in-game** — it was built, deployed (`351,232` bytes, 10:53) and reasoned about, but not played.





























### What changed





























Campaign teams are now written to `MatchMapNode.opponent` **when the map is built**, not when the match launches. The node icon, tooltip, pre-match preview (skaters and colour swatches included) and the match itself therefore all read one TeamData.





























**Hooks** (user chose "implement now with guards" over a probe-first build):





























- **`MapObject.SetOpponents(int act)` postfix — `PatchMapOpponents.AfterSetOpponents`.** This is the primary hook, chosen over `InitializeMap` because `SetOpponents` is *by definition* the point where every elite/boss node has its TeamData. I could not prove from the dump that `InitializeMap` does the assignment itself — `_disasm_func.py` on `InitializeMap` (RVA `0x315E9F0`) resolves **nothing** useful; every call is indirect and the only named targets are a shared thunk. Don't re-attempt that; hook the method whose contract you actually need.














- **`MapObject.InitializeMap` postfix — `PatchMapOpponents.Postfix`.** Backup, in case `SetOpponents` is inlined. Free when the primary ran: every node reports as already current.














- **`Boss/EliteMapNode.LaunchMatch`** now call `CampaignOpponents.Ensure(opponent, Plugin.GamesPlayed, …)` instead of `RemixTeam`. Normally a no-op; it is the **correcting fallback** when map gen couldn't run (loaded map, chaos-regenerated node) or mispredicted. `GamesPlayed` stays the authority for the match that is about to start, so this cannot regress.














- **`PatchMatchPreviewMenu`** kept, but repurposed: it now *corrects* `currentMapNode.opponent` before the player commits, then repaints name + logo (because `ShowMenu` already filled those widgets). It no longer invents display values from `ConfigTeams` independently of the node, so preview and match can no longer disagree.





























**Ordering — "sequential by match depth" (user's decision).** Match nodes are grouped by `Node.LayerIndex`; the layers containing match nodes are ranked ascending; the k-th such layer gets `ConfigTeams[MapStartGamesPlayed + k]`. Branch siblings on one layer deliberately share a team, so **path choice never changes who you play** — same as before this change.





























**New state:** `Plugin.MapStartGamesPlayed`, set in `PatchGenerateNewMap.Prefix`, persisted as a **third field in `save.txt`** (`acts,games,mapStart`). Two-field saves from ≤2.1.31 still load — the third falls back to `GamesPlayed`. It anchors the node→team mapping so it doesn't shift under the player when the map is re-entered.





























`CampaignOpponents` keys applied configs by `TeamData.GetInstanceID()` and is cleared on new map / run end / final boss.





























### Guards, and why each exists





























- **Challenge nodes are skipped.** An unreplaced challenge win does **not** increment `GamesPlayed` (`PatchMatchGameEnd`), so giving it a config team would desynchronise the whole sequence. Challenges the campaign *does* replace are already rewritten to Elite by `PatchCreateMapNode`, so they arrive as real `EliteMapNode`s and are included.














- **`Import Team = PLAYER` is deferred to launch.** The mirror-match path reads the player's team out of `TeamSelection`, which is not populated during map generation. Applying it early would find nothing, log "Import team 'PLAYER' not found", **and then be skipped at launch because the node looked configured**. Both the map pass and the preview pass skip these (`PatchMapOpponents.MustWaitForLaunch`). This was a real regression caught in review, not a hypothetical.














- **Shared-TeamData conflict.** `ActElite.team` / `ActBoss.team` are direct ScriptableObject references, so two layers *could* point at one asset. First layer wins and it warns; the launch path still fixes the match. **Whether this actually happens is still unknown** — watch for `[MapTeams] Layer N shares its TeamData` in the log. If it turns out to be common, distinct-team-per-node is impossible without cloning.














- **Idempotence.** Safe to re-apply: `ApplyTeamFromConfig` wipes powerups/ability/relics before writing, and `CurrentRemixBoost` is `0`, so the one additive step (`BoostTeam`) is a no-op today. If a future change makes the boost non-zero, re-applying would compound it.





























### Performance fix this change forced





























`FindPlayerByName` did an uncached `Resources.FindObjectsOfTypeAll<ForwardData>()` per imported player. That ran a few times per match before; configuring a whole map can now run it ~40× in one frame. Added `AllForwardCache` + `EnsureForwardCache()` with a miss-triggered rescan-and-retry (same recovery pattern as `FindTalent`). The `RANDOM` player path uses it too.





























### What to look for in the log





























```














Patched MapObject.SetOpponents — campaign opponents assigned at map generation














[MapTeams] Map configured from game N (SetOpponents): X match layer(s), ...














[MapTeams] L2=>G1('Blue Jackets (25-26)') L4=>G2(...) ...














```














If you instead see only `[MapTeams] ... (InitializeMap)`, `SetOpponents` was inlined and the backup carried it. If you see `Could not find MapObject.SetOpponents`, the primary hook never registered.





























### Known limitation, stated plainly





























On a **mixed layer** (match nodes and rest nodes side by side) the player can pass a match layer without playing a match, so the depth prediction drifts from `GamesPlayed`. Deeper nodes then advertise the wrong team. The preview pass corrects the node being entered before the player commits, and the launch pass corrects the match, so **what you play is always right** — but a distant node's icon can be wrong until you get near it. Fixing that properly needs the player's real position on the map at map-init time, which was deliberately not attempted here.





























> **MEASURED 2026-08-01 (session 15) — this is far narrower than written above.**














> The user pointed out that skipping a match layer doesn't happen in practice, and














> `_game_maps.txt` confirms it: **all 173 match-bearing layers across all 17 squads














> have a match on EVERY sibling.** No shipped map puts a match beside a rest node,














> so branch choice cannot change how many games you play, and the template-fixed














> nodes cannot drift.














>














> The only remaining path to drift is the **85 layers that are entirely `NULL` with














> 2+ nodes** — types rolled at generation, so one branch could become an Elite while














> its sibling becomes a Shop. Those are exactly the nodes the per-node editor does














> NOT expose (their type isn't known until generation), so they are always on the














> sequential fallback. Don't cite the paragraph above as a general limitation.

> You're picking up the Tape to Tape Custom Campaign Framework — a BepInEx 6 IL2CPP Harmony mod (`src/CustomCampaignFramework/Plugin.cs` ~15k lines, plus `PreviewAssets.cs`) and a Tkinter campaign-creator GUI (`release/Custom Campaigns Mod/creator_gui.py`, ~9.5k lines, frozen with PyInstaller). Working tree is `C:\Users\kiran\Desktop\tape to tape mods`; the GitHub repo under Documents is a mirror written by `_sync_github.ps1`, not the source of truth.














>














> **Read `SESSION_HANDOFF.md` first**, especially session 17 — it cost four launches to get right and the traps are all written down.














>














> **State:** code is **2.1.34**, which is released. The tree is **ahead by two unsynced changes**: the session-16 mirror-match removal and the session-17 game-asset previews. Everything is built and deployed. **2.1.35 is shippable whenever the user wants** — bump the 4 version files (`release/VERSION.txt`, `Plugin.cs` `[BepInPlugin]`, `creator_gui.py` `APP_VERSION`, `installer/T2T_CampaignFramework.iss`), add a CHANGELOG entry, then `_build_all.ps1 -Sync "msg"`.














>














> **Working and confirmed** (session 17 §3d/§3e): the Player Editor shows real game art — 82 face PNGs, 166 equipment previews, an exact full-player live render that persists after the game closes (its request id is a hash of the player config, so a render is reusable forever), and a colour panel showing the configured kit head to skates.














>














> **The one open feature is offline recolouring** (§3f). Rendering each piece isolated, once per colour channel, to get masks the Creator recombines as `base + Σ(mask × colour)` — the code is written on both sides, Pillow is bundled into the exe, the maths is tested against real files. It is **switched off** because the passes produce full coloured skaters instead of masks: **`RefreshSkinAndColors` is an async UniTask that rebuilds the skeleton AFTER `BeginLayerPass` applies the slot isolation and channel colours**, resetting both. The fix is a second settle phase inside the job so they land after the refresh completes. Flip `PreviewAssets.GenerateColorLayers` and `GameAssetPreview._COMPOSITING_ENABLED` when it works. Do NOT re-derive the slot names or the colouring mechanism — both are in §3e/§3f.














>














> **Do not re-litigate:** map-node art is `rewardIcon_image`, a uGUI `Image` (s14). Mirror match must never go through `TeamSelection` (s16 §3). The off-screen preview stage does not work — an instantiated `SkaterPreviewInUI` never builds its canvas renderers (`UseOffScreenStage = false`, s17 §3c).














>














> **House rules learned the hard way:**














> - **Verify renders by CONTENT, not by hash or file count.** Two separate false passes this session: 161 byte-identical blank PNGs reported as successes, and a proof gate that compared hashes while anti-aliasing made four visually identical images differ. `md5sum *.png | sort -u | wc -l` and actually *looking* at an image are what caught both.














> - **Anything that hangs off the render path only runs when something renders.** With a full cache nothing renders, so dumps and layer passes silently never fire. Drive that work from the inventory scan instead.














> - The game is **URP**. `Graphics.DrawMeshNow`/`GL` from `Update()` silently draws nothing; use a `CommandBuffer` + `Graphics.ExecuteCommandBuffer`. `Graphics.Blit` does work. `GL.GetGPUProjectionMatrix` needs `renderIntoTexture: false` or output is upside down.














> - The game's `Spine/T2T SkeletonGraphic` material refuses to draw into a foreign render target — redraw with `UI/Default`.














> - **Never read boxed Il2Cpp structs via IL2CPP reflection** (`Skin.Attachments`): hard crash, 0xC0000005, try/catch will not save you. Reference arrays like `ExposedList<Slot>` are fine — `Slot` is a class.














> - Build with `dotnet build -c Release`; deploy with `_deploy.ps1` / `_deploy_exe.ps1`, or `_build_all.ps1 -Deploy` for everything. **All fail while the game or Creator is running** — check `Get-Process` first, and verify the deployed DLL hash matches a clean rebuild rather than trusting file size (small edits often produce identical sizes).














> - Python is `C:\Users\kiran\AppData\Local\Python\pythoncore-3.14-64\python.exe`. Bare `python` is a Store stub that lies about being missing.














> - **Never pipe `_build_all.ps1` through `2>&1`** — PyInstaller logs to stderr and `$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"` aborts the build.














> - `dump_2026-07-10/dump.cs` is the only valid dump and is signatures-only. Runtime probes through existing patches have been far more productive than disassembly.














> - The user tests by playing, and moves fast. Say plainly when something can't be verified before it ships, and never call deployed-but-unseen work "working".
